### Ereignis 6: Runde 3

- Reviewer: `claude`
- Freigabe: `YES`
- Validierungsbindung: `validation-d15c4ca9e2a3`
- Testdateien: keine
- Prüfdimensionen: Checked dimensions — reviewer-replay fail-closed coverage (log uniqueness, idempotency-key parsing, attestation binding, verdict/round/reviewer identity), resume-exception guard near-miss coverage (ordering, attribution, prefix validity, subset checks), and confirmation that no unrelated/unrequested production scope was added this round beyond the C-08-responsive tightening.
- Größtes Restrisiko: Largest residual risk: the replay mechanism's correctness still depends on an exact string-based idempotency-key format (&#96;parsed:{logical_id}:{fingerprint}:{digest}&#96;) and a fixed log filename convention; a future refactor of either without updating both &#96;recover_pending_reviewer&#96; and its test suite in lockstep could silently disable replay (safe/fail-closed direction) or, in a worse case, admit an ambiguous match if uniqueness assumptions about log rotation are violated in production but not represented in tests.
- Realistische Bruchbedingung: Break condition: a future change to provider log retention/rotation produces two attempt logs with identical stripped output content for different logical reviewer invocations within the same work unit/round, which the current uniqueness check would treat as ambiguous and correctly refuse (fail closed) — the realistic break condition is therefore operational (workflow halts) rather than a security bypass, and no test currently proves that halted state has a documented, safe manual-recovery path in the runbook.
- Eigene Findings: `C-01`, `C-02`, `C-03`, `C-04`, `C-05`, `C-06`, `C-07`, `C-08`

### Ereignis 8: Runde 4

- Reviewer: `claude`
- Freigabe: `YES`
- Validierungsbindung: `validation-5674eac5bb56`
- Testdateien: `tests/test_agent_runtime.py`, `tests/test_artifact_migration.py`, `tests/test_git_service.py`, `tests/test_structured_artifact_regressions.py`, `tests/test_workflow.py`, `tests/test_workflow_state.py`
- Prüfdimensionen: Checked dimensions — replay/resume fail-closed near-miss coverage (identity, round, verdict, idempotency-key parsing, attestation binding, log uniqueness, partial mirror), correctness and safety of the new automatic Claude-rewind-on-stale-fingerprint path, absence of unrequested/unreviewed new production scope beyond the closed C-01–C-08 findings, and confirmation via the recorded unexpected-file gate that the interim "Recovery-Hotfix" did not alter the already-reviewed C-05 negative-path tests.
- Größtes Restrisiko: Largest residual risk: the replay/resume mechanisms remain dependent on an exact string-based idempotency-key format (&#96;parsed:{logical_id}:{fingerprint}:{digest}&#96;) and a fixed log-filename convention; a future refactor of either surface without updating &#96;recover_pending_reviewer&#96; and its guard functions in lockstep could silently disable replay (safe, fail-closed direction) rather than corrupt state.
- Realistische Bruchbedingung: Realistic break condition: a change to provider log retention/rotation or idempotency-key construction elsewhere in the codebase causes legitimate resume/replay attempts to fall back to fail-closed denial (&#96;WorkflowExecutionError&#96;/&#96;ArtifactResumeError&#96;) more often than intended; this is an operational availability risk, not a security bypass, and remains undocumented in the operator runbook — acceptable as residual risk, not a blocker.
- Eigene Findings: `C-01`, `C-02`, `C-03`, `C-04`, `C-05`, `C-06`, `C-07`, `C-08`

### Ereignis 9: Runde 1

- Reviewer: `antigravity`
- Freigabe: `YES`
- Validierungsbindung: `validation-5674eac5bb56`
- Testdateien: `tests/test_agent_runtime.py`, `tests/test_artifact_migration.py`, `tests/test_git_service.py`, `tests/test_structured_artifact_regressions.py`, `tests/test_workflow.py`, `tests/test_workflow_state.py`
- Prüfdimensionen: Checked dimensions — fail-closed adapter validation (rejection of unregistered adapters before preparation), fail-closed reviewer-replay integrity (exact SHA-256 attestation binding, single unique provider log hash match, strict idempotency-key prefix format, round and reviewer identity checks), resume-exception guards in artifact_migration.py (ordering, attribution subsets, prefix validity, and duplicate review rejection), commit-authorization drift negative paths (stale approved head commit, broken ancestry, and inexact approved external paths), and automatic Claude-rewind on fingerprint change ahead of Antigravity review.
- Größtes Restrisiko: Largest residual risk: replay and resume exception logic relies on exact string-based idempotency keys and log filename conventions; refactoring or changing log retention without simultaneous updates to recover_pending_reviewer and resume guards could silently disable deterministic replay and trigger fail-closed denials during interrupted-checkpoint resumes.
- Realistische Bruchbedingung: Realistic break condition: an operator or background task purges or modifies provider log files while a work unit is in an interrupted review state, causing recover_pending_reviewer to find no unique hash-matching log and halting with WorkflowExecutionError rather than silently bypassing review integrity.
- Eigene Findings: keine

- Realistische Bruchbedingung: Realistic break condition: an external system or test environment changes the dao-{provider}-runtime- prefix structure or temp directory layout without updating adapter path canonicalization, resulting in spurious ProviderInputMeasurementPayload revisions across identical resume attempts rather than an authorization bypass.

#### Work Unit 02 – Slice 01

- Auftrag: Verlustfreie Providerinput-Messung und Startbarriere
- Scope: `docs/internal/phase-2-arbeitspaket-1-review-bootstrap-implement-review-cc745616.md`, `docs/internal/slice-phase-2-arbeitspaket-1-review-bootstrap-arbeitsplan-01-verlustfreie-providerinput-messung-und-startbarriere.md`, `orchestrator.toml`, `src/agent_adapters.py`, `src/agent_runtime.py`, `src/cli.py`, `src/orchestrator.py`, `src/provider_input_budget.py`, `tests/test_agent_adapters.py`, `tests/test_agent_runtime.py`, `tests/test_cli.py`, `tests/test_provider_input_budget.py`

- Teständerungsfreigabe: nicht erfasst.
- Pre-Mortems:
  - Ereignis 2: In three months, the most likely failure is a new or renamed &#96;WorkflowStep&#96;/adapter added without updating &#96;provider_input_budget.PROVIDER_OPERATIONS&#96; or the &#96;orchestrator.toml&#96; budget table in lockstep, so a specific step either crashes with an unclassified &#96;ProviderInputBudgetError&#96; deep in &#96;measure_provider_input&#96; (breaking that workflow step outright) or, for a differently-named adapter, silently bypasses the size barrier altogether — both are quiet regressions of the exact invariant this slice was built to guarantee, and neither is currently caught by an explicit completeness test.
  - Ereignis 3: In three months, the most likely failure mode is an adapter refactor or new CLI argument carrying prompt data that is omitted from _provider_input_components, resulting in undercounted provider input and subtle context window overflow at runtime.

#### Work Unit 03 – Slice 02

- Auftrag: Fingerprintgebundene Checks und Finalreview-Preflight
- Scope: `docs/internal/phase-2-arbeitspaket-1-review-bootstrap-implement-review-cc745616.md`, `docs/internal/slice-phase-2-arbeitspaket-1-review-bootstrap-arbeitsplan-02-fingerprintgebundene-checks-und-finalreview-preflight.md`, `schemas/orchestrator-artifact-v1.schema.json`, `src/artifact_bridge.py`, `src/artifact_migration.py`, `src/artifact_models.py`, `src/artifact_projection.py`, `src/final_review_preflight.py`, `src/orchestrator.py`, `src/workflow.py`, `src/workflow_state.py`, `tests/test_artifact_bridge.py`, `tests/test_artifact_migration.py`, `tests/test_artifact_models.py`, `tests/test_artifact_projection.py`, `tests/test_final_review_preflight.py`, `tests/test_orchestrator_runtime.py`, `tests/test_structured_artifact_regressions.py`, `tests/test_workflow.py`, `tests/test_workflow_state.py`

- Teständerungsfreigabe: nicht erfasst.
- Pre-Mortems:
  - Ereignis 2: In three months, the most likely failure is a future edit to &#96;agent_runtime.py&#96; (or new logging/telemetry) that reads &#96;.measurement&#96; off any caught &#96;ProviderInputBudgetExceeded&#96;, unaware that &#96;FinalReviewPreflightDenied&#96; is a same-typed subclass whose constructor never sets that attribute — producing an &#96;AttributeError&#96; exactly at the moment a final-review preflight denial should instead cleanly halt the workflow.
  - Ereignis 3: In three months, the most likely failure mode is an addition of a new bootstrap check record type or transition fact that is omitted from relevant_record_head's _BOOTSTRAP_TYPES filter, causing circular dependency in transition fingerprints and false-positive dual-write verification mismatches during resume.

#### Work Unit 04 – Slice 03

- Auftrag: Resume, Watch, Audit und Betriebsdokumentation
- Scope: `Quickstart.md`, `README.md`, `docs/internal/phase-2-arbeitspaket-1-review-bootstrap-implement-review-cc745616.md`, `docs/internal/slice-phase-2-arbeitspaket-1-review-bootstrap-arbeitsplan-03-resume-watch-audit-und-betriebsdokumentation.md`, `src/artifact_projection.py`, `src/inbox_watcher.py`, `src/orchestrator.py`, `tests/test_dry_run_scenarios.py`, `tests/test_inbox_watcher.py`, `tests/test_language_consistency.py`, `tests/test_orchestrator_watch_cli.py`, `tests/test_structured_artifact_regressions.py`, `workflow.puml`

- Teständerungsfreigabe: nicht erfasst.
- Pre-Mortems:
  - Ereignis 3: In three months, the most likely failure is exactly the C-05 scenario — someone refactors &#96;quota_resume_diff_acknowledgement&#96; or the tuple-literal &#96;acknowledged&#96; expression in &#96;_revalidate_waiting_diff&#96; in isolation (they live in different files/functions with no shared type contract visible in either diff), silently breaking the write/read match; a QUOTA-RESUME-DIFF gate would then never clear even after correct human approval, forcing every quota-interrupted run with a coincidental repo change to loop on the same gate indefinitely until someone traces the mismatch by hand.
  - Ereignis 4: In three months, the most likely failure cause is an extension of the inbox watcher or queue management that introduces a new non-zero exit code or gate reason for automated checks, but neglects to explicitly handle the exit-4 / BOOTSTRAP_CHECK invariant in WatchTaskResult.__post_init__, triggering a ValueError during queue dispatch.
  - Ereignis 6: In three months, the most likely failure is that a future gated correction approves &#96;approved_external_paths&#96; for one repository state, but an additional unreviewed edit lands on one of those already-approved external paths (or a genuinely new external path) before the slice commit executes; because the only regression coverage for this authorization path is the single happy-path test, this silent divergence between "what was reviewed" and "what commit_slice is about to commit" is not caught by an explicit failing test today, and would surface only if a maintainer manually traces a GitTransactionError or, worse, an over-permissive path match.
  - Ereignis 7: In three months, the most likely failure cause is an update to the host OS or container environment that introduces a non-standard zoneinfo path or stripped timezone symlink format not recognized by _system_local_timezone, causing Codex dated local reset timestamps to fail parsing and fall back to manual exit 2 instead of automatic quota sleep.

  - Ereignis 8: In three months, the most likely failure is operational rather than logical: a log-retention or idempotency-key format change desynchronizes from what &#96;recover_pending_reviewer&#96;/the two &#96;artifact_migration.py&#96; guards parse, causing a legitimate interrupted-checkpoint resume to halt fail-closed instead of replaying, requiring a manual state inspection that isn't yet documented in the runbook — not a silent bypass of review integrity.
  - Ereignis 9: In three months, the most probable failure scenario is an operational stall rather than a security defect: changes to provider logging, log file retention, or idempotency-key string schemas could cause resume-time recovery to fail closed with WorkflowExecutionError or ArtifactResumeError, requiring operator intervention and state inspection that is not yet fully detailed in the runbook.

#### Work Unit 02 – Slice 01

- Auftrag: Verlustfreie Providerinput-Messung und Startbarriere
- Scope: `docs/internal/phase-2-arbeitspaket-1-review-bootstrap-implement-review-cc745616.md`, `docs/internal/slice-phase-2-arbeitspaket-1-review-bootstrap-arbeitsplan-01-verlustfreie-providerinput-messung-und-startbarriere.md`, `orchestrator.toml`, `src/agent_adapters.py`, `src/agent_runtime.py`, `src/cli.py`, `src/orchestrator.py`, `src/provider_input_budget.py`, `tests/test_agent_adapters.py`, `tests/test_agent_runtime.py`, `tests/test_cli.py`, `tests/test_provider_input_budget.py`

### `C-01` — `OPEN`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 1
- Klasse: `OBSERVATION`
- Finding: The &#96;operation&#96; strings passed from &#96;orchestrator.py&#96; (&#96;invocation.step.value&#96;) into &#96;run_agent&#96;/&#96;measure_provider_input&#96; are matched against &#96;provider_input_budget.PROVIDER_OPERATIONS&#96; only implicitly, via the fact that the full suite currently passes; there is no dedicated completeness test asserting that every &#96;WorkflowStep&#96; value used at each &#96;invoke_codex&#96;/&#96;invoke_reviewer&#96;/&#96;repair_review_contract&#96; call site is a member of &#96;PROVIDER_OPERATIONS[provider]&#96;. A future renamed or added &#96;WorkflowStep&#96; without a matching budget-table entry would make &#96;ProviderInputBudgetPolicy.select()&#96; raise an unclassified &#96;ProviderInputBudgetError&#96; (not the deterministic &#96;ProviderInputBudgetExceeded&#96; denial path), propagating as an unclassified crash instead of a clean, documented bootstrap denial.
- Akzeptanztest: Add a unit test (e.g. in tests/test_orchestrator_runtime.py or tests/test_provider_input_budget.py, whichever slice owns orchestrator wiring next) that iterates every WorkflowStep value used for codex/claude/antigravity invocations and asserts each resolves via &#96;default_provider_input_budget_policy().select(provider, provider, step.value)&#96; without raising.
- Statusbegründung: –

### `C-02` — `OPEN`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 1
- Klasse: `OBSERVATION`
- Finding: In &#96;run_agent&#96;, budget measurement/enforcement is gated by &#96;if agent_key in PROVIDER_OPERATIONS&#96;, i.e. by adapter &#96;.name&#96; string equality; an adapter whose &#96;.name&#96; is not literally "codex"/"claude"/"antigravity" (a future fourth provider, or a renamed adapter) silently skips the input-size barrier entirely rather than failing closed, which is inconsistent with the "resume is fail-closed" posture stated for structured artifacts elsewhere in the contract. Currently low risk since only these three adapters exist and are exercised.
- Akzeptanztest: When a new provider/adapter is introduced, require either its explicit inclusion in &#96;PROVIDER_OPERATIONS&#96; before it can run unmeasured, or an assertion in &#96;run_agent&#96;/its tests that every registered adapter name is a key of &#96;PROVIDER_OPERATIONS&#96;.
- Statusbegründung: –

### `C-01` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 1
- Klasse: `OBSERVATION`
- Finding: The &#96;operation&#96; strings passed from &#96;orchestrator.py&#96; (&#96;invocation.step.value&#96;) into &#96;run_agent&#96;/&#96;measure_provider_input&#96; are matched against &#96;provider_input_budget.PROVIDER_OPERATIONS&#96; only implicitly, via the fact that the full suite currently passes; there is no dedicated completeness test asserting that every &#96;WorkflowStep&#96; value used at each &#96;invoke_codex&#96;/&#96;invoke_reviewer&#96;/&#96;repair_review_contract&#96; call site is a member of &#96;PROVIDER_OPERATIONS[provider]&#96;. A future renamed or added &#96;WorkflowStep&#96; without a matching budget-table entry would make &#96;ProviderInputBudgetPolicy.select()&#96; raise an unclassified &#96;ProviderInputBudgetError&#96; (not the deterministic &#96;ProviderInputBudgetExceeded&#96; denial path), propagating as an unclassified crash instead of a clean, documented bootstrap denial.

### `C-06` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 1

This directly contradicts the stated invariant "Historische Läufe ohne Protokollbindung bleiben im Modus legacy-state-v3 ... sie werden weder still migriert noch benötigen sie nachträglich Records" — the new bootstrap barrier silently imposes a structured-v1-only requirement onto legacy final review, permanently blocking it instead of leaving it on its prior contract.
- Akzeptanztest: VALIDATE: ["python3", "-m", "pytest", "tests/", "-v"]
- Statusbegründung: Legacy-state-v3 runs now persist the budget fact and skip the structured-v1-only record-chain preflight instead of permanently denying with ATTESTATION-MISSING; covered by a dedicated regression test asserting no artifact directory is created.

All five dimensions named in the original finding (verdict, open_findings subset, attestation fingerprint, work-unit kinds, missing-signature count) each have a dedicated failure mode, and both directions of the open_findings-subset check (state side and record side) are covered. ### `C-08` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 2
- Klasse: `BLOCKER`
- Finding: This correction round's only reported open finding was C-07, scoped to adding negative-path tests for the existing &#96;_recoverable_final_denial_mirror_gap&#96; guard. The delivered diff, however, also introduces a substantial new, previously-absent reviewer-replay subsystem that was never requested by C-07: &#96;workflow.PersistedReviewerReplay&#96;, &#96;ProductionWorkflowDriver.recover_pending_reviewer&#96; (src/orchestrator.py), and a new branch inserted ahead of the normal reviewer-invocation path in &#96;WorkflowEngine&#96; (src/workflow.py) that, when its conditions match, skips invoking the actual reviewer process entirely and instead synthesizes a denied review result purely from a historical structured &#96;ReviewPayload&#96; record plus an on-disk provider log file matched only by SHA-256 output-digest equality and an &#96;idempotency_key&#96; prefix parse (&#96;f"parsed:{logical_id}:{fingerprint}:"&#96;). It also adds two further fail-open-relevant guards reused for resume comparison, &#96;_recoverable_pending_correction_record&#96; and &#96;_recoverable_pending_review_finding_gap&#96; (src/artifact_migration.py). Unlike &#96;_recoverable_final_denial_mirror_gap&#96;, which now has 8 dedicated near-miss/negative tests after this round, this new replay machinery has exactly one happy-path integration test (&#96;test_hash_bound_denied_review_replays_after_checkpoint_failure_without_provider&#96;) and zero negative-path tests proving it stays fail-closed when, e.g., two provider log files hash-match the same digest, the persisted record's round number, reviewer identity, or verdict is off by one from what &#96;recover_pending_reviewer&#96; expects, the idempotency-key prefix is malformed, or the state-v3 mirror already partially (but not fully) reflects the event. Introducing unreviewed, untested, security/fail-closed-sensitive scope well beyond the one reported blocker, inside what the contract defines as a narrow convergence round, is itself an actionable defect: it was neither requested by an open finding nor exercised by comparable adversarial coverage, and a real checkpoint/log-matching edge case here could let the workflow accept a synthesized review decision without ever re-invoking the live reviewer.
- Akzeptanztest: VALIDATE: ["python3", "-m", "pytest", "tests/", "-v"]
- Statusbegründung: Codex delivered exactly the negative-path coverage C-08 required: 6 parametrized near-miss tests for &#96;recover_pending_reviewer&#96; (reviewer identity, round number, verdict, idempotency-key prefix, partial state-v3 mirror, attestation fingerprint) plus a dedicated non-unique-hash-log rejection test, and 7+7 near-miss tests for the two &#96;artifact_migration.py&#96; resume-exception guards (&#96;_recoverable_pending_correction_record&#96;, &#96;_recoverable_pending_review_finding_gap&#96;) covering identity, verdict, attribution, ordering, and prefix/uniqueness. Additionally the replay path now requires an exact-fingerprint orchestrator attestation match, a genuine fail-closed tightening. All traced logic paths reject the enumerated near-misses via explicit early returns, and the full suite (900 tests) is bound to this fingerprint. No remaining gap identified.

- Statusbegründung: Legacy-state-v3 final review now skips the structured-only preflight instead of permanently denying, with regression coverage confirming the corrected behavior.

### `C-08` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 2
- Klasse: `BLOCKER`
- Finding: This correction round's only reported open finding was C-07, scoped to adding negative-path tests for the existing &#96;_recoverable_final_denial_mirror_gap&#96; guard. The delivered diff, however, also introduces a substantial new, previously-absent reviewer-replay subsystem that was never requested by C-07: &#96;workflow.PersistedReviewerReplay&#96;, &#96;ProductionWorkflowDriver.recover_pending_reviewer&#96; (src/orchestrator.py), and a new branch inserted ahead of the normal reviewer-invocation path in &#96;WorkflowEngine&#96; (src/workflow.py) that, when its conditions match, skips invoking the actual reviewer process entirely and instead synthesizes a denied review result purely from a historical structured &#96;ReviewPayload&#96; record plus an on-disk provider log file matched only by SHA-256 output-digest equality and an &#96;idempotency_key&#96; prefix parse (&#96;f"parsed:{logical_id}:{fingerprint}:"&#96;). It also adds two further fail-open-relevant guards reused for resume comparison, &#96;_recoverable_pending_correction_record&#96; and &#96;_recoverable_pending_review_finding_gap&#96; (src/artifact_migration.py). Unlike &#96;_recoverable_final_denial_mirror_gap&#96;, which now has 8 dedicated near-miss/negative tests after this round, this new replay machinery has exactly one happy-path integration test (&#96;test_hash_bound_denied_review_replays_after_checkpoint_failure_without_provider&#96;) and zero negative-path tests proving it stays fail-closed when, e.g., two provider log files hash-match the same digest, the persisted record's round number, reviewer identity, or verdict is off by one from what &#96;recover_pending_reviewer&#96; expects, the idempotency-key prefix is malformed, or the state-v3 mirror already partially (but not fully) reflects the event. Introducing unreviewed, untested, security/fail-closed-sensitive scope well beyond the one reported blocker, inside what the contract defines as a narrow convergence round, is itself an actionable defect: it was neither requested by an open finding nor exercised by comparable adversarial coverage, and a real checkpoint/log-matching edge case here could let the workflow accept a synthesized review decision without ever re-invoking the live reviewer.
- Akzeptanztest: VALIDATE: ["python3", "-m", "pytest", "tests/", "-v"]
- Statusbegründung: The new reviewer-replay/resume-exception machinery has dense near-miss fail-closed coverage.

### `C-11` — `OPEN`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 1
- Klasse: `BLOCKER`
- Finding: In &#96;run_final_review_preflight&#96;, the &#96;BindingPayload&#96; validation loop (&#96;for item in records: ... if isinstance(item.payload, BindingPayload): references = (item.payload.attestation_id, *item.payload.approval_ids); missing = ...; if any(records_by_id[ref].fingerprint != item.fingerprint for ref in references): deny FINGERPRINT-MISMATCH&#96;) verifies only that each referenced &#96;attestation_id&#96;/&#96;approval_ids&#96; record-id (a) exists in the current chain and (b) shares the binding record's exact fingerprint. It never checks that the referenced record actually carries the expected payload type (&#96;ValidationAttestationPayload&#96; for &#96;attestation_id&#96;, &#96;ReviewPayload&#96; for each &#96;approval_ids&#96; entry), nor that it precedes the binding in the chain. This matters because this codebase routinely shares one &#96;fingerprint.sha256&#96; across many unrelated record types within the same transition — the bound decision tables in this very packet show &#96;task&#96;, &#96;plan&#96;, &#96;agent_result&#96;, &#96;finding_transition&#96;, &#96;validation_attestation&#96;, and &#96;review&#96; records all carrying the identical &#96;implementation:&lt;hash&gt;&#96; fingerprint inside one work unit. A corrupted, partially restored, or hand-repaired record chain (the exact recovery scenario the contract's "resume is fail-closed" invariant exists to guard: "Missing, corrupt, unknown, or mirror-divergent structured records require restoring the matching chain or mirror before continuation") in which &#96;attestation_id&#96; happens to resolve to any non-&#96;ValidationAttestationPayload&#96; record sharing that fingerprint (e.g. a &#96;finding_transition&#96; or &#96;agent_result&#96;), or an &#96;approval_ids&#96; entry resolves to any non-&#96;ReviewPayload&#96; record with that fingerprint, silently passes this check instead of failing closed with &#96;MISSING-REFERENCE&#96;/&#96;FINGERPRINT-MISMATCH&#96;. This is precisely the new agent-free integrity barrier meant to catch such divergence immediately before Codex/Claude/Antigravity final-review invocation, so the gap is in the newly delivered code path itself, not merely residual/theoretical risk elsewhere. Existing tests (&#96;tests/test_final_review_preflight.py&#96;) only exercise the positive path (&#96;_append_external_path_evidence&#96;) and the "missing reference"/"wrong fingerprint" happy-path denials indirectly via &#96;ATTESTATION-MISSING&#96;/&#96;UNAUTHORIZED-PATH&#96;; none constructs a chain where a &#96;BindingPayload&#96; reference resolves to a same-fingerprint record of the wrong payload type.
- Akzeptanztest: VALIDATE: ["python3", "-m", "pytest", "tests/", "-v"]
- Statusbegründung: –

- Akzeptanztest: Add a unit test (e.g. in tests/test_orchestrator_runtime.py or tests/test_provider_input_budget.py, whichever slice owns orchestrator wiring next) that iterates every WorkflowStep value used for codex/claude/antigravity invocations and asserts each resolves via &#96;default_provider_input_budget_policy().select(provider, provider, step.value)&#96; without raising.
- Statusbegründung: Included in the closed C-01..C-08 range validated by the bound attestation validation-dbf79f200984 (903 passed); no residual defect identified for this finding in this final review.

### `C-02` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 1
- Klasse: `BLOCKER`
- Finding: In &#96;run_agent&#96;, budget measurement/enforcement is gated by &#96;if agent_key in PROVIDER_OPERATIONS&#96;, i.e. by adapter &#96;.name&#96; string equality; an adapter whose &#96;.name&#96; is not literally "codex"/"claude"/"antigravity" (a future fourth provider, or a renamed adapter) silently skips the input-size barrier entirely rather than failing closed, which is inconsistent with the "resume is fail-closed" posture stated for structured artifacts elsewhere in the contract. Currently low risk since only these three adapters exist and are exercised.
- Akzeptanztest: When a new provider/adapter is introduced, require either its explicit inclusion in &#96;PROVIDER_OPERATIONS&#96; before it can run unmeasured, or an assertion in &#96;run_agent&#96;/its tests that every registered adapter name is a key of &#96;PROVIDER_OPERATIONS&#96;.
- Statusbegründung: Adapter registration is fail-closed, with dedicated regression coverage confirmed by the bound attestation.

### `C-03` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 1
- Klasse: `OBSERVATION`
- Finding: The riskiest new logic — &#96;orchestrator.py::_persist_provider_bootstrap&#96;'s ordered dual-write (measurement → state mirror → preflight → state mirror) and &#96;workflow.py&#96;'s new &#96;except (ProviderInputBudgetExceeded, FinalReviewPreflightDenied)&#96; branch that sets &#96;GateReason.BOOTSTRAP_CHECK&#96; and checkpoints — is only exercised indirectly by pre-existing happy-path integration tests; no test forces an actual denial through the real driver/engine to confirm the gate, checkpoint, and idempotent resume/retry behavior.
- Akzeptanztest: Add an integration test (tests/test_orchestrator_runtime.py or tests/test_workflow.py) that forces &#96;ProviderInputBudgetExceeded&#96; or &#96;FinalReviewPreflightDenied&#96; through &#96;ProductionWorkflowDriver&#96;/&#96;WorkflowEngine&#96;'s real invocation path and asserts the resulting state has &#96;current_work_unit.gate.reason is GateReason.BOOTSTRAP_CHECK&#96;, a checkpoint was written, and resuming does not duplicate the already-persisted measurement/preflight records.
- Statusbegründung: A real driver/engine budget-denial persists gate/checkpoint/idempotent measurement across resume, per dedicated regression coverage.

### `C-04` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 1
- Klasse: `BLOCKER`
- Finding: In tests/test_workflow_state.py, the new test &#96;test_bootstrap_facts_roundtrip_idempotently_and_use_a_resume_gate&#96; was spliced in without a blank line before the trailing three assertions of &#96;test_init_workflow_state_uses_v3_and_one_based_ids&#96;, so those assertions (&#96;max_codex_returns&#96;, &#96;gate.status&#96;, &#96;branch_base&#96;) now silently run inside the new function against its own unrelated &#96;state&#96; object; the original test lost that coverage and the new test carries confusing unrelated assertions.
- Akzeptanztest: Separate the two test bodies with a blank line so each function only asserts its own invariants, and confirm &#96;test_init_workflow_state_uses_v3_and_one_based_ids&#96; still independently asserts &#96;max_codex_returns&#96;, &#96;gate.status is CLEAR&#96;, and &#96;branch_base&#96;.
- Statusbegründung: The misplaced test assertions were fixed.

### `C-05` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 2
- Klasse: `BLOCKER`
- Finding: The new HEAD-drift commit-authorization path in &#96;commit_slice&#96;/&#96;CommitAuthorization&#96; (approved_head_commit + approved_external_paths, ancestry check, exact-set path check) is a security-sensitive loosening of the prior absolute "HEAD must equal persisted start_commit" invariant, but only one happy-path integration test exists (&#96;test_commit_accepts_exactly_approved_descendant_head_and_commits_only_worktree&#96; in tests/test_git_service.py). No visible test forces the negative paths: (a) HEAD advanced but &#96;authorization.approved_head_commit&#96; is stale/mismatched, (b) &#96;start_commit&#96; is not an ancestor of HEAD (e.g. after a rewrite/reset), or (c) &#96;authorization.approved_external_paths&#96; under- or over-approves the actual unexpected paths in the reviewed diff. Any of these should still raise &#96;GitTransactionError&#96;, but that is currently only implied by code reading, not proven by a test.
- Akzeptanztest: Add negative-path tests in tests/test_git_service.py asserting &#96;commit_slice&#96; raises &#96;GitTransactionError&#96; for: a HEAD descendant with a stale/mismatched &#96;approved_head_commit&#96;; a &#96;start_commit&#96; that is not an ancestor of the current HEAD; and an &#96;approved_external_paths&#96; set that does not exactly equal the unexpected out-of-scope paths present in the reviewed diff.
- Statusbegründung: The HEAD-drift commit-authorization negative paths exist and are covered by dedicated regression tests.

### `C-06` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 1
- Klasse: `BLOCKER`
- Finding: &#96;ProductionWorkflowDriver._persist_provider_bootstrap&#96; unconditionally requires an ArtifactBridge-backed record chain to pass the new final-review preflight: when &#96;self._artifact_bridge is None&#96; (legacy-state-v3 runs without a persisted protocol binding), &#96;chain = ()&#96;, a synthetic unpersisted &#96;ArtifactRecord&#96; is used for the measurement, and &#96;run_final_review_preflight&#96; can never find a matching &#96;ValidationAttestationPayload&#96; in an always-empty chain — so &#96;codex_final_review&#96;/&#96;claude_final_review&#96;/&#96;antigravity_final_review&#96; for any legacy-state-v3 run permanently denies with &#96;ATTESTATION-MISSING&#96;, with no remediation path that clears it (re-running validation does not populate a chain that structurally cannot exist). This directly contradicts the stated invariant "Historische Läufe ohne Protokollbindung bleiben im Modus legacy-state-v3 ... sie werden weder still migriert noch benötigen sie nachträglich Records" — the new bootstrap barrier silently imposes a structured-v1-only requirement onto legacy final review, permanently blocking it instead of leaving it on its prior contract.
- Akzeptanztest: VALIDATE: ["python3", "-m", "pytest", "tests/", "-v"]
- Statusbegründung: Legacy-state-v3 final review now skips the structured-only preflight instead of permanently denying, with regression coverage confirming the corrected behavior.

### `C-07` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 1
- Klasse: `BLOCKER`
- Finding: &#96;orchestrator.py::_recoverable_final_denial_mirror_gap&#96;, wired into the structured-decision/state-v3-mirror consistency guard, is a newly introduced relaxation of the fail-closed "records must match the mirror" invariant with only one happy-path test and no negative-path coverage proving it still fails closed when the verdict, open_findings subset, attestation fingerprint, work-unit kinds, or missing-signature count deviate even slightly from the exact recognized pattern.
- Akzeptanztest: VALIDATE: ["python3", "-m", "pytest", "tests/", "-v"]
- Statusbegründung: The mirror-gap guard has 8 near-miss negative tests covering the previously reported gap.

### `C-08` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 2
- Klasse: `BLOCKER`
- Finding: This correction round's only reported open finding was C-07, scoped to adding negative-path tests for the existing &#96;_recoverable_final_denial_mirror_gap&#96; guard. The delivered diff, however, also introduces a substantial new, previously-absent reviewer-replay subsystem that was never requested by C-07: &#96;workflow.PersistedReviewerReplay&#96;, &#96;ProductionWorkflowDriver.recover_pending_reviewer&#96; (src/orchestrator.py), and a new branch inserted ahead of the normal reviewer-invocation path in &#96;WorkflowEngine&#96; (src/workflow.py) that, when its conditions match, skips invoking the actual reviewer process entirely and instead synthesizes a denied review result purely from a historical structured &#96;ReviewPayload&#96; record plus an on-disk provider log file matched only by SHA-256 output-digest equality and an &#96;idempotency_key&#96; prefix parse (&#96;f"parsed:{logical_id}:{fingerprint}:"&#96;). It also adds two further fail-open-relevant guards reused for resume comparison, &#96;_recoverable_pending_correction_record&#96; and &#96;_recoverable_pending_review_finding_gap&#96; (src/artifact_migration.py). Unlike &#96;_recoverable_final_denial_mirror_gap&#96;, which now has 8 dedicated near-miss/negative tests after this round, this new replay machinery has exactly one happy-path integration test (&#96;test_hash_bound_denied_review_replays_after_checkpoint_failure_without_provider&#96;) and zero negative-path tests proving it stays fail-closed when, e.g., two provider log files hash-match the same digest, the persisted record's round number, reviewer identity, or verdict is off by one from what &#96;recover_pending_reviewer&#96; expects, the idempotency-key prefix is malformed, or the state-v3 mirror already partially (but not fully) reflects the event. Introducing unreviewed, untested, security/fail-closed-sensitive scope well beyond the one reported blocker, inside what the contract defines as a narrow convergence round, is itself an actionable defect: it was neither requested by an open finding nor exercised by comparable adversarial coverage, and a real checkpoint/log-matching edge case here could let the workflow accept a synthesized review decision without ever re-invoking the live reviewer.
- Akzeptanztest: VALIDATE: ["python3", "-m", "pytest", "tests/", "-v"]
- Statusbegründung: The new reviewer-replay/resume-exception machinery has dense near-miss fail-closed coverage.

### `C-09` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 1
- Klasse: `BLOCKER`
- Finding: Claude provider-input measurement is not deterministic/idempotent across repeated invocations of an identical logical review. &#96;ClaudeAdapter._provider_input_components&#96;'s &#96;packet_manifest&#96; component now embeds each packet file's full absolute path (&#96;f"- \&#96;{packet_file}\&#96;
- Akzeptanztest: ..."&#96;, changed in this branch from the prior &#96;packet_file.name&#96;), and the model-facing start directive references that same absolute manifest path; the corresponding tests were updated to assert the full path is present. Because the runtime directory is freshly generated per invocation, this makes &#96;input_digest&#96; — and therefore &#96;transition_fingerprint&#96;, the &#96;provider-input-*&#96; idempotency_key, and the persisted &#96;bootstrap_checks&#96; semantic digest — differ every time the identical review is resumed or replayed, silently producing a new &#96;provider_input_measurement&#96; revision instead of the idempotent no-op that Slice 01's own report claims ("Zufällige lokale Transportpfade aus modellwirksamen Direktiven entfernt, damit unveränderte Aufträge stabile Digests erhalten") and that README/Antigravity's Slice-01 review ("deterministic payload hashing") assert. The only closed acceptance test for bootstrap idempotency (C-03) never exercises a second real Claude invocation through a fresh runtime directory, so this regression is unexercised. &#124; VALIDATE: ["python3", "-m", "pytest", "tests/", "-v"]
- Statusbegründung: Claude runtime-path canonicalization (length-preserving placeholder + literal replace across all measured components) makes repeated preparations of an identical prompt produce identical components and identical input_digest, verified end-to-end via a dedicated two-invocation regression test that sweeps every component for residual runtime-path leakage; verified in the bound 905-test attestation.

### `C-10` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 1
- Klasse: `BLOCKER`
- Finding: &#96;run_final_review_preflight&#96;'s authorized-path computation (&#96;allowed_paths = set(state.task_scope_patterns)
- Akzeptanztest: ...&#96;) matches &#96;repository_paths&#96; against &#96;task_scope_patterns&#96; by literal set membership rather than glob/pattern semantics, so a task or Slice legitimately scoped with a non-literal pattern (e.g. &#96;src/**&#96;) will have its real changed paths (e.g. &#96;src/foo.py&#96;) incorrectly reported as &#96;UNAUTHORIZED-PATH&#96;, halting the branch-wide final review even though the change is inside the declared scope. Antigravity already flagged exactly this risk as a residual/break-condition in the Slice 02 review ("a task definition using non-literal glob expressions in TASK_SCOPE triggers UNAUTHORIZED-PATH ... despite matching task scope patterns"), but it was never converted into a tracked finding or fixed, and it remains live in the new hard preflight barrier gating every final-review provider call. &#124; VALIDATE: ["python3", "-m", "pytest", "tests/", "-v"]
- Statusbegründung: The final-review preflight's authorized-path check now uses the same centrally shared POSIX glob matcher used elsewhere in the codebase for task/slice scope, while keeping externally approved paths exact-set bound; a dedicated regression test proves a &#96;src/**&#96;-scoped path is now accepted, and the pre-existing unauthorized-path denial test still passes, confirmed in the bound 905-test attestation.

### `C-11` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 1
- Klasse: `BLOCKER`
- Finding: In &#96;run_final_review_preflight&#96;, the &#96;BindingPayload&#96; validation loop (&#96;for item in records: ... if isinstance(item.payload, BindingPayload): references = (item.payload.attestation_id, *item.payload.approval_ids); missing = ...; if any(records_by_id[ref].fingerprint != item.fingerprint for ref in references): deny FINGERPRINT-MISMATCH&#96;) verifies only that each referenced &#96;attestation_id&#96;/&#96;approval_ids&#96; record-id (a) exists in the current chain and (b) shares the binding record's exact fingerprint. It never checks that the referenced record actually carries the expected payload type (&#96;ValidationAttestationPayload&#96; for &#96;attestation_id&#96;, &#96;ReviewPayload&#96; for each &#96;approval_ids&#96; entry), nor that it precedes the binding in the chain. This matters because this codebase routinely shares one &#96;fingerprint.sha256&#96; across many unrelated record types within the same transition — the bound decision tables in this very packet show &#96;task&#96;, &#96;plan&#96;, &#96;agent_result&#96;, &#96;finding_transition&#96;, &#96;validation_attestation&#96;, and &#96;review&#96; records all carrying the identical &#96;implementation:&lt;hash&gt;&#96; fingerprint inside one work unit. A corrupted, partially restored, or hand-repaired record chain (the exact recovery scenario the contract's "resume is fail-closed" invariant exists to guard: "Missing, corrupt, unknown, or mirror-divergent structured records require restoring the matching chain or mirror before continuation") in which &#96;attestation_id&#96; happens to resolve to any non-&#96;ValidationAttestationPayload&#96; record sharing that fingerprint (e.g. a &#96;finding_transition&#96; or &#96;agent_result&#96;), or an &#96;approval_ids&#96; entry resolves to any non-&#96;ReviewPayload&#96; record with that fingerprint, silently passes this check instead of failing closed with &#96;MISSING-REFERENCE&#96;/&#96;FINGERPRINT-MISMATCH&#96;. This is precisely the new agent-free integrity barrier meant to catch such divergence immediately before Codex/Claude/Antigravity final-review invocation, so the gap is in the newly delivered code path itself, not merely residual/theoretical risk elsewhere. Existing tests (&#96;tests/test_final_review_preflight.py&#96;) only exercise the positive path (&#96;_append_external_path_evidence&#96;) and the "missing reference"/"wrong fingerprint" happy-path denials indirectly via &#96;ATTESTATION-MISSING&#96;/&#96;UNAUTHORIZED-PATH&#96;; none constructs a chain where a &#96;BindingPayload&#96; reference resolves to a same-fingerprint record of the wrong payload type.
- Akzeptanztest: VALIDATE: ["python3", "-m", "pytest", "tests/", "-v"]
- Statusbegründung: The BindingPayload validation loop now rejects both wrong-payload-type references (attestation must resolve to ValidationAttestationPayload, each approval_id to ReviewPayload) and references that do not precede the binding in the record chain, via record_positions[ref] &gt;= index. Two dedicated negative-path regression tests (wrong-type parametrized over attestation/approval, and non-predecessor via chain-order swap) cover the exact gap described in this finding, and the fail-closed MISSING-REFERENCE denial is verified end-to-end in the bound 908-test attestation for this fingerprint.

This directly contradicts the stated invariant "Historische Läufe ohne Protokollbindung bleiben im Modus legacy-state-v3 ... sie werden weder still migriert noch benötigen sie nachträglich Records" — the new bootstrap barrier silently imposes a structured-v1-only requirement onto legacy final review, permanently blocking it instead of leaving it on its prior contract.

- 119. `ar1-a9443748c59b585e5e17a9d847460adb737892c3e2887d93e2522b2d6454e468`: `C-07` `responded` durch `codex`; `BLOCKER` / `open` — ACCEPTED: Der Mirror-Gap-Guard wird jetzt für alle geforderten Near-Miss-Abweichungen fail-closed getestet, einschließlich des autoritativen Record-Chain-Pfads.
- 125. `ar1-7787a8615719103dc15649f63b55b9887f61bd055fcba78108eaad691c9e0140`: `C-07` `status_changed` durch `claude`; `BLOCKER` / `closed` — Codex delivered exactly the required near-miss negative-path tests for &#96;_recoverable_final_denial_mirror_gap&#96; (&#96;test_external_side_effect_guard_rejects_near_miss_final_denial_recovery&#96;, 8 parametrized &#96;failure_mode&#96;s: approved-verdict, state-open-findings-not-subset, record-open-findings-not-subset, attestation-fingerprint, reviewed-unit-kind, correction-unit-kind, multiple-missing-signatures, mirror-ahead) plus the positive happy-path test (&#96;test_external_side_effect_guard_accepts_legacy_final_denial_correction_transition&#96;). All five dimensions named in the original finding (verdict, open_findings subset, attestation fingerprint, work-unit kinds, missing-signature count) each have a dedicated failure mode, and both directions of the open_findings-subset check (state side and record side) are covered. Verified in the bound validation-14f23d429a42 attestation (879 passed).
- 126. `ar1-3f4486d9ff5c5a25cf19b26090863d35446dd011655841b8a4dc7c1100d17af8`: `C-08` `opened` durch `claude`; `BLOCKER` / `open` — This correction round's only reported open finding was C-07, scoped to adding negative-path tests for the existing &#96;_recoverable_final_denial_mirror_gap&#96; guard. The delivered diff, however, also introduces a substantial new, previously-absent reviewer-replay subsystem that was never requested by C-07: &#96;workflow.PersistedReviewerReplay&#96;, &#96;ProductionWorkflowDriver.recover_pending_reviewer&#96; (src/orchestrator.py), and a new branch inserted ahead of the normal reviewer-invocation path in &#96;WorkflowEngine&#96; (src/workflow.py) that, when its conditions match, skips invoking the actual reviewer process entirely and instead synthesizes a denied review result purely from a historical structured &#96;ReviewPayload&#96; record plus an on-disk provider log file matched only by SHA-256 output-digest equality and an &#96;idempotency_key&#96; prefix parse (&#96;f"parsed:{logical_id}:{fingerprint}:"&#96;). It also adds two further fail-open-relevant guards reused for resume comparison, &#96;_recoverable_pending_correction_record&#96; and &#96;_recoverable_pending_review_finding_gap&#96; (src/artifact_migration.py). Unlike &#96;_recoverable_final_denial_mirror_gap&#96;, which now has 8 dedicated near-miss/negative tests after this round, this new replay machinery has exactly one happy-path integration test (&#96;test_hash_bound_denied_review_replays_after_checkpoint_failure_without_provider&#96;) and zero negative-path tests proving it stays fail-closed when, e.g., two provider log files hash-match the same digest, the persisted record's round number, reviewer identity, or verdict is off by one from what &#96;recover_pending_reviewer&#96; expects, the idempotency-key prefix is malformed, or the state-v3 mirror already partially (but not fully) reflects the event. Introducing unreviewed, untested, security/fail-closed-sensitive scope well beyond the one reported blocker, inside what the contract defines as a narrow convergence round, is itself an actionable defect: it was neither requested by an open finding nor exercised by comparable adversarial coverage, and a real checkpoint/log-matching edge case here could let the workflow accept a synthesized review decision without ever re-invoking the live reviewer.
- 130. `ar1-4b2b779192f23e7cb4303018fecde8e6d6795aa21f9b012c50d0296d4ba41e3a`: `C-08` `responded` durch `codex`; `BLOCKER` / `open` — ACCEPTED: Replay-Identität, Runde, Verdict, Idempotenzbindung, Attestierung, Log-Eindeutigkeit, Teil-Mirror sowie beide Resume-Ausnahmen besitzen nun negative Fail-closed-Abdeckung.
- 136. `ar1-019338cf220fbf6d33819d79c369fd1aacd376ade6a734bb1b13f8bf1ac2c534`: `C-08` `status_changed` durch `claude`; `BLOCKER` / `closed` — Codex delivered exactly the negative-path coverage C-08 required: 6 parametrized near-miss tests for &#96;recover_pending_reviewer&#96; (reviewer identity, round number, verdict, idempotency-key prefix, partial state-v3 mirror, attestation fingerprint) plus a dedicated non-unique-hash-log rejection test, and 7+7 near-miss tests for the two &#96;artifact_migration.py&#96; resume-exception guards (&#96;_recoverable_pending_correction_record&#96;, &#96;_recoverable_pending_review_finding_gap&#96;) covering identity, verdict, attribution, ordering, and prefix/uniqueness. Additionally the replay path now requires an exact-fingerprint orchestrator attestation match, a genuine fail-closed tightening. All traced logic paths reject the enumerated near-misses via explicit early returns, and the full suite (900 tests) is bound to this fingerprint. No remaining gap identified.

- 158. `ar1-a5f9cff337cafe20f6034ca1cdb810c93cdb39969842912166f89e2db2b68e12`: `C-10` `opened` durch `claude`; `BLOCKER` / `open` — &#96;run_final_review_preflight&#96;'s authorized-path computation (&#96;allowed_paths = set(state.task_scope_patterns)
- 162. `ar1-5fb85b2dc5df25ca51955ba7ce1edf59cf49e80eea51bde97f794d2a4b0174e8`: `C-09` `responded` durch `codex`; `BLOCKER` / `open` — ACCEPTED: Zufällige Claude-Runtime-Suffixe werden längengleich kanonisiert; identische Reviews erzeugen über frische Runtime-Verzeichnisse stabile Input-Digests.
- 163. `ar1-92e4e50e61aaa02b428f397ee4dfcbffa37b45c04a5219a4d50608d1db664774`: `C-10` `responded` durch `codex`; `BLOCKER` / `open` — ACCEPTED: Der Finalreview-Preflight verwendet nun die zentrale POSIX-Glob-Semantik für Task- und Slice-Scope; extern genehmigte Pfade bleiben exakt gebunden.
- 168. `ar1-26e6c40997a9dab64e7037cf245ffc1957e687584bde56ddb7d4b720f78dce2f`: `C-09` `status_changed` durch `claude`; `BLOCKER` / `closed` — Claude runtime-path canonicalization (length-preserving placeholder + literal replace across all measured components) makes repeated preparations of an identical prompt produce identical components and identical input_digest, verified end-to-end via a dedicated two-invocation regression test that sweeps every component for residual runtime-path leakage; verified in the bound 905-test attestation.
- 169. `ar1-12f7843085ee6866d769807434f4d5c78a84b06093d5886432647464b227d33b`: `C-10` `status_changed` durch `claude`; `BLOCKER` / `closed` — The final-review preflight's authorized-path check now uses the same centrally shared POSIX glob matcher used elsewhere in the codebase for task/slice scope, while keeping externally approved paths exact-set bound; a dedicated regression test proves a &#96;src/**&#96;-scoped path is now accepted, and the pre-existing unauthorized-path denial test still passes, confirmed in the bound 905-test attestation.
- 184. `ar1-40edf3792096a273a92d37bec7e6498303664ef6f35206619abf98c9b786d424`: `C-11` `opened` durch `claude`; `BLOCKER` / `open` — In &#96;run_final_review_preflight&#96;, the &#96;BindingPayload&#96; validation loop (&#96;for item in records: ... if isinstance(item.payload, BindingPayload): references = (item.payload.attestation_id, *item.payload.approval_ids); missing = ...; if any(records_by_id[ref].fingerprint != item.fingerprint for ref in references): deny FINGERPRINT-MISMATCH&#96;) verifies only that each referenced &#96;attestation_id&#96;/&#96;approval_ids&#96; record-id (a) exists in the current chain and (b) shares the binding record's exact fingerprint. It never checks that the referenced record actually carries the expected payload type (&#96;ValidationAttestationPayload&#96; for &#96;attestation_id&#96;, &#96;ReviewPayload&#96; for each &#96;approval_ids&#96; entry), nor that it precedes the binding in the chain. This matters because this codebase routinely shares one &#96;fingerprint.sha256&#96; across many unrelated record types within the same transition — the bound decision tables in this very packet show &#96;task&#96;, &#96;plan&#96;, &#96;agent_result&#96;, &#96;finding_transition&#96;, &#96;validation_attestation&#96;, and &#96;review&#96; records all carrying the identical &#96;implementation:&lt;hash&gt;&#96; fingerprint inside one work unit. A corrupted, partially restored, or hand-repaired record chain (the exact recovery scenario the contract's "resume is fail-closed" invariant exists to guard: "Missing, corrupt, unknown, or mirror-divergent structured records require restoring the matching chain or mirror before continuation") in which &#96;attestation_id&#96; happens to resolve to any non-&#96;ValidationAttestationPayload&#96; record sharing that fingerprint (e.g. a &#96;finding_transition&#96; or &#96;agent_result&#96;), or an &#96;approval_ids&#96; entry resolves to any non-&#96;ReviewPayload&#96; record with that fingerprint, silently passes this check instead of failing closed with &#96;MISSING-REFERENCE&#96;/&#96;FINGERPRINT-MISMATCH&#96;. This is precisely the new agent-free integrity barrier meant to catch such divergence immediately before Codex/Claude/Antigravity final-review invocation, so the gap is in the newly delivered code path itself, not merely residual/theoretical risk elsewhere. Existing tests (&#96;tests/test_final_review_preflight.py&#96;) only exercise the positive path (&#96;_append_external_path_evidence&#96;) and the "missing reference"/"wrong fingerprint" happy-path denials indirectly via &#96;ATTESTATION-MISSING&#96;/&#96;UNAUTHORIZED-PATH&#96;; none constructs a chain where a &#96;BindingPayload&#96; reference resolves to a same-fingerprint record of the wrong payload type.
- 188. `ar1-b2158a91f4e65eddef61855a4180133dff053d2d1b4919656c46a3b6f3a2be87`: `C-11` `responded` durch `codex`; `BLOCKER` / `open` — ACCEPTED: Binding-Attestierungs- und Freigabereferenzen erfordern nun typkorrekte vorausgehende Records; Negativtests decken falsche Payload-Typen und nachgelagerte Referenzen ab.
- 195. `ar1-10b01fcba3a103b2d4e08db1853f6454a543fe38a3690e9a1921e6ba2476b1a8`: `C-11` `status_changed` durch `claude`; `BLOCKER` / `closed` — The BindingPayload validation loop now rejects both wrong-payload-type references (attestation must resolve to ValidationAttestationPayload, each approval_id to ReviewPayload) and references that do not precede the binding in the record chain, via record_positions[ref] &gt;= index. Two dedicated negative-path regression tests (wrong-type parametrized over attestation/approval, and non-predecessor via chain-order swap) cover the exact gap described in this finding, and the fail-closed MISSING-REFERENCE denial is verified end-to-end in the bound 908-test attestation for this fingerprint.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| C-01 | claude | The &#96;operation&#96; strings passed from &#96;orchestrator.py&#96; (&#96;invocation.step.value&#96;) into &#96;run_agent&#96;/&#96;measure_provider_input&#96; are matched against &#96;provider_input_budget.PROVIDER_OPERATIONS&#96; only implicitly, via the fact that the full suite currently passes; there is no dedicated completeness test asserting that every &#96;WorkflowStep&#96; value used at each &#96;invoke_codex&#96;/&#96;invoke_reviewer&#96;/&#96;repair_review_contract&#96; call site is a member of &#96;PROVIDER_OPERATIONS[provider]&#96;. A future renamed or added &#96;WorkflowStep&#96; without a matching budget-table entry would make &#96;ProviderInputBudgetPolicy.select()&#96; raise an unclassified &#96;ProviderInputBudgetError&#96; (not the deterministic &#96;ProviderInputBudgetExceeded&#96; denial path), propagating as an unclassified crash instead of a clean, documented bootstrap denial. | OBSERVATION | offen | offen |
| C-02 | claude | In &#96;run_agent&#96;, budget measurement/enforcement is gated by &#96;if agent_key in PROVIDER_OPERATIONS&#96;, i.e. by adapter &#96;.name&#96; string equality; an adapter whose &#96;.name&#96; is not literally "codex"/"claude"/"antigravity" (a future fourth provider, or a renamed adapter) silently skips the input-size barrier entirely rather than failing closed, which is inconsistent with the "resume is fail-closed" posture stated for structured artifacts elsewhere in the contract. Currently low risk since only these three adapters exist and are exercised. | OBSERVATION | offen | offen |

| C-02 | claude | In &#96;run_agent&#96;, budget measurement/enforcement is gated by &#96;if agent_key in PROVIDER_OPERATIONS&#96;, i.e. by adapter &#96;.name&#96; string equality; an adapter whose &#96;.name&#96; is not literally "codex"/"claude"/"antigravity" (a future fourth provider, or a renamed adapter) silently skips the input-size barrier entirely rather than failing closed, which is inconsistent with the "resume is fail-closed" posture stated for structured artifacts elsewhere in the contract. Currently low risk since only these three adapters exist and are exercised. | OBSERVATION | bestritten | offen |
| C-03 | claude | The riskiest new logic — &#96;orchestrator.py::_persist_provider_bootstrap&#96;'s ordered dual-write (measurement → state mirror → preflight → state mirror) and &#96;workflow.py&#96;'s new &#96;except (ProviderInputBudgetExceeded, FinalReviewPreflightDenied)&#96; branch that sets &#96;GateReason.BOOTSTRAP_CHECK&#96; and checkpoints — is only exercised indirectly by pre-existing happy-path integration tests; no test forces an actual denial through the real driver/engine to confirm the gate, checkpoint, and idempotent resume/retry behavior. | OBSERVATION | offen | offen |
| C-04 | claude | In tests/test_workflow_state.py, the new test &#96;test_bootstrap_facts_roundtrip_idempotently_and_use_a_resume_gate&#96; was spliced in without a blank line before the trailing three assertions of &#96;test_init_workflow_state_uses_v3_and_one_based_ids&#96;, so those assertions (&#96;max_codex_returns&#96;, &#96;gate.status&#96;, &#96;branch_base&#96;) now silently run inside the new function against its own unrelated &#96;state&#96; object; the original test lost that coverage and the new test carries confusing unrelated assertions. | OBSERVATION | offen | offen |

| C-06 | claude | &#96;ProductionWorkflowDriver._persist_provider_bootstrap&#96; unconditionally requires an ArtifactBridge-backed record chain to pass the new final-review preflight: when &#96;self._artifact_bridge is None&#96; (legacy-state-v3 runs without a persisted protocol binding), &#96;chain = ()&#96;, a synthetic unpersisted &#96;ArtifactRecord&#96; is used for the measurement, and &#96;run_final_review_preflight&#96; can never find a matching &#96;ValidationAttestationPayload&#96; in an always-empty chain — so &#96;codex_final_review&#96;/&#96;claude_final_review&#96;/&#96;antigravity_final_review&#96; for any legacy-state-v3 run permanently denies with &#96;ATTESTATION-MISSING&#96;, with no remediation path that clears it (re-running validation does not populate a chain that structurally cannot exist). This directly contradicts the stated invariant "Historische Läufe ohne Protokollbindung bleiben im Modus legacy-state-v3 ... sie werden weder still migriert noch benötigen sie nachträglich Records" — the new bootstrap barrier silently imposes a structured-v1-only requirement onto legacy final review, permanently blocking it instead of leaving it on its prior contract. | BLOCKER | angenommen | erledigt: Legacy-state-v3 runs now persist the budget fact and skip the structured-v1-only record-chain preflight instead of permanently denying with ATTESTATION-MISSING; covered by a dedicated regression test asserting no artifact directory is created. |
| C-07 | claude | &#96;orchestrator.py::_recoverable_final_denial_mirror_gap&#96;, wired into the structured-decision/state-v3-mirror consistency guard, is a newly introduced relaxation of the fail-closed "records must match the mirror" invariant with only one happy-path test and no negative-path coverage proving it still fails closed when the verdict, open_findings subset, attestation fingerprint, work-unit kinds, or missing-signature count deviate even slightly from the exact recognized pattern. | BLOCKER | angenommen | erledigt: Codex delivered exactly the required near-miss negative-path tests for &#96;_recoverable_final_denial_mirror_gap&#96; (&#96;test_external_side_effect_guard_rejects_near_miss_final_denial_recovery&#96;, 8 parametrized &#96;failure_mode&#96;s: approved-verdict, state-open-findings-not-subset, record-open-findings-not-subset, attestation-fingerprint, reviewed-unit-kind, correction-unit-kind, multiple-missing-signatures, mirror-ahead) plus the positive happy-path test (&#96;test_external_side_effect_guard_accepts_legacy_final_denial_correction_transition&#96;). All five dimensions named in the original finding (verdict, open_findings subset, attestation fingerprint, work-unit kinds, missing-signature count) each have a dedicated failure mode, and both directions of the open_findings-subset check (state side and record side) are covered. Verified in the bound validation-14f23d429a42 attestation (879 passed). |
| C-08 | claude | This correction round's only reported open finding was C-07, scoped to adding negative-path tests for the existing &#96;_recoverable_final_denial_mirror_gap&#96; guard. The delivered diff, however, also introduces a substantial new, previously-absent reviewer-replay subsystem that was never requested by C-07: &#96;workflow.PersistedReviewerReplay&#96;, &#96;ProductionWorkflowDriver.recover_pending_reviewer&#96; (src/orchestrator.py), and a new branch inserted ahead of the normal reviewer-invocation path in &#96;WorkflowEngine&#96; (src/workflow.py) that, when its conditions match, skips invoking the actual reviewer process entirely and instead synthesizes a denied review result purely from a historical structured &#96;ReviewPayload&#96; record plus an on-disk provider log file matched only by SHA-256 output-digest equality and an &#96;idempotency_key&#96; prefix parse (&#96;f"parsed:{logical_id}:{fingerprint}:"&#96;). It also adds two further fail-open-relevant guards reused for resume comparison, &#96;_recoverable_pending_correction_record&#96; and &#96;_recoverable_pending_review_finding_gap&#96; (src/artifact_migration.py). Unlike &#96;_recoverable_final_denial_mirror_gap&#96;, which now has 8 dedicated near-miss/negative tests after this round, this new replay machinery has exactly one happy-path integration test (&#96;test_hash_bound_denied_review_replays_after_checkpoint_failure_without_provider&#96;) and zero negative-path tests proving it stays fail-closed when, e.g., two provider log files hash-match the same digest, the persisted record's round number, reviewer identity, or verdict is off by one from what &#96;recover_pending_reviewer&#96; expects, the idempotency-key prefix is malformed, or the state-v3 mirror already partially (but not fully) reflects the event. Introducing unreviewed, untested, security/fail-closed-sensitive scope well beyond the one reported blocker, inside what the contract defines as a narrow convergence round, is itself an actionable defect: it was neither requested by an open finding nor exercised by comparable adversarial coverage, and a real checkpoint/log-matching edge case here could let the workflow accept a synthesized review decision without ever re-invoking the live reviewer. | BLOCKER | angenommen | erledigt: Codex delivered exactly the negative-path coverage C-08 required: 6 parametrized near-miss tests for &#96;recover_pending_reviewer&#96; (reviewer identity, round number, verdict, idempotency-key prefix, partial state-v3 mirror, attestation fingerprint) plus a dedicated non-unique-hash-log rejection test, and 7+7 near-miss tests for the two &#96;artifact_migration.py&#96; resume-exception guards (&#96;_recoverable_pending_correction_record&#96;, &#96;_recoverable_pending_review_finding_gap&#96;) covering identity, verdict, attribution, ordering, and prefix/uniqueness. Additionally the replay path now requires an exact-fingerprint orchestrator attestation match, a genuine fail-closed tightening. All traced logic paths reject the enumerated near-misses via explicit early returns, and the full suite (900 tests) is bound to this fingerprint. No remaining gap identified. |

No visible test forces the negative paths: (a) HEAD advanced but &#96;authorization.approved_head_commit&#96; is stale/mismatched, (b) &#96;start_commit&#96; is not an ancestor of HEAD (e.g. after a rewrite/reset), or (c) &#96;authorization.approved_external_paths&#96; under- or over-approves the actual unexpected paths in the reviewed diff. Any of these should still raise &#96;GitTransactionError&#96;, but that is currently only implied by code reading, not proven by a test. | BLOCKER | bestritten | erledigt: The HEAD-drift commit-authorization negative paths exist and are covered by dedicated regression tests. |
| C-06 | claude | &#96;ProductionWorkflowDriver._persist_provider_bootstrap&#96; unconditionally requires an ArtifactBridge-backed record chain to pass the new final-review preflight: when &#96;self._artifact_bridge is None&#96; (legacy-state-v3 runs without a persisted protocol binding), &#96;chain = ()&#96;, a synthetic unpersisted &#96;ArtifactRecord&#96; is used for the measurement, and &#96;run_final_review_preflight&#96; can never find a matching &#96;ValidationAttestationPayload&#96; in an always-empty chain — so &#96;codex_final_review&#96;/&#96;claude_final_review&#96;/&#96;antigravity_final_review&#96; for any legacy-state-v3 run permanently denies with &#96;ATTESTATION-MISSING&#96;, with no remediation path that clears it (re-running validation does not populate a chain that structurally cannot exist). This directly contradicts the stated invariant "Historische Läufe ohne Protokollbindung bleiben im Modus legacy-state-v3 ... sie werden weder still migriert noch benötigen sie nachträglich Records" — the new bootstrap barrier silently imposes a structured-v1-only requirement onto legacy final review, permanently blocking it instead of leaving it on its prior contract. | BLOCKER | angenommen | erledigt: Legacy-state-v3 final review now skips the structured-only preflight instead of permanently denying, with regression coverage confirming the corrected behavior. |
| C-07 | claude | &#96;orchestrator.py::_recoverable_final_denial_mirror_gap&#96;, wired into the structured-decision/state-v3-mirror consistency guard, is a newly introduced relaxation of the fail-closed "records must match the mirror" invariant with only one happy-path test and no negative-path coverage proving it still fails closed when the verdict, open_findings subset, attestation fingerprint, work-unit kinds, or missing-signature count deviate even slightly from the exact recognized pattern. | BLOCKER | angenommen | erledigt: The mirror-gap guard has 8 near-miss negative tests covering the previously reported gap. |
| C-08 | claude | This correction round's only reported open finding was C-07, scoped to adding negative-path tests for the existing &#96;_recoverable_final_denial_mirror_gap&#96; guard. The delivered diff, however, also introduces a substantial new, previously-absent reviewer-replay subsystem that was never requested by C-07: &#96;workflow.PersistedReviewerReplay&#96;, &#96;ProductionWorkflowDriver.recover_pending_reviewer&#96; (src/orchestrator.py), and a new branch inserted ahead of the normal reviewer-invocation path in &#96;WorkflowEngine&#96; (src/workflow.py) that, when its conditions match, skips invoking the actual reviewer process entirely and instead synthesizes a denied review result purely from a historical structured &#96;ReviewPayload&#96; record plus an on-disk provider log file matched only by SHA-256 output-digest equality and an &#96;idempotency_key&#96; prefix parse (&#96;f"parsed:{logical_id}:{fingerprint}:"&#96;). It also adds two further fail-open-relevant guards reused for resume comparison, &#96;_recoverable_pending_correction_record&#96; and &#96;_recoverable_pending_review_finding_gap&#96; (src/artifact_migration.py). Unlike &#96;_recoverable_final_denial_mirror_gap&#96;, which now has 8 dedicated near-miss/negative tests after this round, this new replay machinery has exactly one happy-path integration test (&#96;test_hash_bound_denied_review_replays_after_checkpoint_failure_without_provider&#96;) and zero negative-path tests proving it stays fail-closed when, e.g., two provider log files hash-match the same digest, the persisted record's round number, reviewer identity, or verdict is off by one from what &#96;recover_pending_reviewer&#96; expects, the idempotency-key prefix is malformed, or the state-v3 mirror already partially (but not fully) reflects the event. Introducing unreviewed, untested, security/fail-closed-sensitive scope well beyond the one reported blocker, inside what the contract defines as a narrow convergence round, is itself an actionable defect: it was neither requested by an open finding nor exercised by comparable adversarial coverage, and a real checkpoint/log-matching edge case here could let the workflow accept a synthesized review decision without ever re-invoking the live reviewer. | BLOCKER | angenommen | erledigt: The new reviewer-replay/resume-exception machinery has dense near-miss fail-closed coverage. |
| C-09 | claude | Claude provider-input measurement is not deterministic/idempotent across repeated invocations of an identical logical review. &#96;ClaudeAdapter._provider_input_components&#96;'s &#96;packet_manifest&#96; component now embeds each packet file's full absolute path (&#96;f"- \&#96;{packet_file}\&#96; | BLOCKER | offen | offen |
| C-10 | claude | &#96;run_final_review_preflight&#96;'s authorized-path computation (&#96;allowed_paths = set(state.task_scope_patterns) | BLOCKER | offen | offen |

| C-10 | claude | &#96;run_final_review_preflight&#96;'s authorized-path computation (&#96;allowed_paths = set(state.task_scope_patterns) | BLOCKER | angenommen | erledigt: The final-review preflight's authorized-path check now uses the same centrally shared POSIX glob matcher used elsewhere in the codebase for task/slice scope, while keeping externally approved paths exact-set bound; a dedicated regression test proves a &#96;src/**&#96;-scoped path is now accepted, and the pre-existing unauthorized-path denial test still passes, confirmed in the bound 905-test attestation. |
| C-11 | claude | In &#96;run_final_review_preflight&#96;, the &#96;BindingPayload&#96; validation loop (&#96;for item in records: ... if isinstance(item.payload, BindingPayload): references = (item.payload.attestation_id, *item.payload.approval_ids); missing = ...; if any(records_by_id[ref].fingerprint != item.fingerprint for ref in references): deny FINGERPRINT-MISMATCH&#96;) verifies only that each referenced &#96;attestation_id&#96;/&#96;approval_ids&#96; record-id (a) exists in the current chain and (b) shares the binding record's exact fingerprint. It never checks that the referenced record actually carries the expected payload type (&#96;ValidationAttestationPayload&#96; for &#96;attestation_id&#96;, &#96;ReviewPayload&#96; for each &#96;approval_ids&#96; entry), nor that it precedes the binding in the chain. This matters because this codebase routinely shares one &#96;fingerprint.sha256&#96; across many unrelated record types within the same transition — the bound decision tables in this very packet show &#96;task&#96;, &#96;plan&#96;, &#96;agent_result&#96;, &#96;finding_transition&#96;, &#96;validation_attestation&#96;, and &#96;review&#96; records all carrying the identical &#96;implementation:&lt;hash&gt;&#96; fingerprint inside one work unit. A corrupted, partially restored, or hand-repaired record chain (the exact recovery scenario the contract's "resume is fail-closed" invariant exists to guard: "Missing, corrupt, unknown, or mirror-divergent structured records require restoring the matching chain or mirror before continuation") in which &#96;attestation_id&#96; happens to resolve to any non-&#96;ValidationAttestationPayload&#96; record sharing that fingerprint (e.g. a &#96;finding_transition&#96; or &#96;agent_result&#96;), or an &#96;approval_ids&#96; entry resolves to any non-&#96;ReviewPayload&#96; record with that fingerprint, silently passes this check instead of failing closed with &#96;MISSING-REFERENCE&#96;/&#96;FINGERPRINT-MISMATCH&#96;. This is precisely the new agent-free integrity barrier meant to catch such divergence immediately before Codex/Claude/Antigravity final-review invocation, so the gap is in the newly delivered code path itself, not merely residual/theoretical risk elsewhere. Existing tests (&#96;tests/test_final_review_preflight.py&#96;) only exercise the positive path (&#96;_append_external_path_evidence&#96;) and the "missing reference"/"wrong fingerprint" happy-path denials indirectly via &#96;ATTESTATION-MISSING&#96;/&#96;UNAUTHORIZED-PATH&#96;; none constructs a chain where a &#96;BindingPayload&#96; reference resolves to a same-fingerprint record of the wrong payload type. | BLOCKER | offen | offen |

| C-01 | claude | The &#96;operation&#96; strings passed from &#96;orchestrator.py&#96; (&#96;invocation.step.value&#96;) into &#96;run_agent&#96;/&#96;measure_provider_input&#96; are matched against &#96;provider_input_budget.PROVIDER_OPERATIONS&#96; only implicitly, via the fact that the full suite currently passes; there is no dedicated completeness test asserting that every &#96;WorkflowStep&#96; value used at each &#96;invoke_codex&#96;/&#96;invoke_reviewer&#96;/&#96;repair_review_contract&#96; call site is a member of &#96;PROVIDER_OPERATIONS[provider]&#96;.
